U^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.37212pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(h)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N(h)+1}}A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.37212pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(h)=A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.37212pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(h)\frac{1}{\sqrt{N(h)}}\,.

(315)

Thus, we can set

\displaystyle A_{A+}(h_{2})U^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.55363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.22008pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.68509pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.68509pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09242pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.15pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}(h_{f})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{N(h_{2})}U^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.55363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.22008pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}(h_{2},h_{f})

\displaystyle U^{{{\dagger d}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.37212pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.54239pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.54239pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.2695pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0527pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}(h_{i})A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.37212pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(h_{1})

\displaystyle=U^{{{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.59233pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.59233pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34946pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.18604pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(h_{i},h_{1})\sqrt{N(h_{1})}\,.

(316)

We therefore find

\displaystyle A_{A+}(h_{2})U^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.37212pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.68509pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.68509pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.09242pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.15pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}(\gamma)A^{{{B}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.1459pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.37212pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.39645pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(h_{1})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{N(h_{2})}U^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.55363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.22008pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}(h_{2},h_{f})t^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.81798pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.81798pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.72401pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80249pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74062pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.55363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.22008pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}(\gamma(n))U^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.8656pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.8656pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81862pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81757pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.81798pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.81798pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.72401pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80249pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}(\gamma_{c})t^{{{c}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.59233pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.59233pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34946pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.18604pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.8656pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.8656pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81862pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81757pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{d}}}(\gamma(1))U^{{{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.59233pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.59233pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34946pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.18604pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(h_{i},h_{1})\sqrt{N(h_{1})}

(317)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{N(h_{2})}U^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(\Gamma_{c})\sqrt{N(h_{1})}\,,

where
\Gamma_{c}
is the corner trajectory defined by first going through
h_{1}
, then through
\gamma
, and finally through
h_{2}
, as shown in
Fig.

27
.
Putting these results together, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(\gamma_{1},h_{1})\cdot\mathbf{E}(\gamma_{2},h_{2})=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{N(h_{1})}U^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(\Gamma_{c}^{-1})N(h_{2})U^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(\Gamma_{c})\sqrt{N(h_{1})}-\frac{N(h_{1})}{2}\quantity(\frac{N(h_2)}{2}+1)\,.

(318)

One can further see that while
U(\Gamma_{c})
creates a Schwinger boson on
h_{2}
and annihilates one on
h_{1}
,
U(\Gamma_{c})
annihilates one on
h_{2}
and creates one on
h_{1}
.
Therefore, this can be simplified to (
79
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}(\gamma_{1},h_{1})\cdot\mathbf{E}(\gamma_{2},h_{2})=U^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(\Gamma_{c}^{-1})U^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.97504pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7767pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.4pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}(\Gamma_{c})\frac{N(h_{1})}{2}(N(h_{2})+1)-\frac{N(h_{1})}{2}\quantity(\frac{N(h_2)}{2}+1)\,.

(319)

In this way we have succeeded at describing the electric Hamiltonian solely in terms of LSH operators as well.

Appendix D
Derivation of the Wave Function

As shown in
Eq.

53
, the basis states of a leaf (or a loop in maximal tree gauge fixing) are given by

\displaystyle\ket{n,l,m}\coloneq\ell_{++}^{n}\mathscr{L}_{++}^{l}(a_{1}^{\dagger}(d))^{l+m}(a_{2}^{\dagger}(d))^{l-m}\ket{0}\,,

(320)

where
n
counts the internal chromoelectric flux while
l
the external.

To derive the corresponding wave functions, we first note that we can use the binomial theorem in order to expand the product of the loop operators in terms of Schwinger Bosons

\displaystyle\ell^{n}_{++}\mathscr{L}^{l}_{++}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{p,q=0}^{l}(-1)^{n+l+k+p+q}\binom{n}{k}\binom{l}{p}\binom{l}{q}\,a^{\dagger}_{1}(a)^{k+p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(a)^{n+l-k-p}a^{\dagger}_{1}(b)^{n+l-k-q}a^{\dagger}_{2}(b)^{k+q}

(321)

\displaystyle\times a^{\dagger}_{1}(c)^{l+q-p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(c)^{l+p-q}\,.

Next, we note that, other than the combinatorial factors, the dependence on
p
and
q
is only through the combinations
k+p
and
k+q
.
We can thus shift these variables so as to isolate the
k
-dependence

\displaystyle\ell^{n}_{++}\mathscr{L}^{l}_{++}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q}\sum_{k}(-1)^{k}\quantity[\sum_k(-1)^k\binom{n}{k}\binom{l}{p-k}\binom{l}{q-k}]\binom{l}{p-k}\binom{l}{q-k}(-1)^{n+l+p+q}\times

(322)

\displaystyle a^{\dagger}_{1}(a)^{p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(a)^{n+l-p}a^{\dagger}_{1}(b)^{n+l-q}a^{\dagger}_{2}(b)^{q}a^{\dagger}_{1}(c)^{l+q-p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(c)^{l+p-q}\,.

When switching the order of summation, the limits become complicated and we have therefore left them implicit in this formula.
They are however completely determined by the requirement that no factorials of negative numbers are taken within the combinatorial factors.

At this stage we recognize that the term in parenthesis looks extremely similar to a Clebsch-Gordan coefficient (e.g. section 8.2.4 in
[
97
]
). Introducing these coefficients then allows us to eliminate the sum over
k

\displaystyle\ell^{n}_{++}\mathscr{L}^{l}_{++}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q}(-1)^{n+l+p+q}\binom{n+l}{n}^{-1}\sqrt{\binom{n+2l+1}{n}\binom{n+l}{p}\binom{n+l}{n+l-q}\binom{2l}{2l+p-q}}

(323)

\displaystyle\times\innerproduct{\frac{n+l}{2},\frac{n+l}{2}-p;\frac{n+l}{2},q-\frac{n+l}{2}}{l,q-p}a^{\dagger}_{1}(a)^{p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(a)^{n+l-p}a^{\dagger}_{1}(b)^{n+l-q}a^{\dagger}_{2}(b)^{q}

\displaystyle\times a^{\dagger}_{1}(c)^{l+q-p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(c)^{l+p-q}\,.

The remaining binomial coefficients that appear when doing this greatly simplify against the normalization factors in (
21
), so that when acting on the vacuum state, this operator creates

\displaystyle\ell^{n}_{++}\mathscr{L}^{l}_{++}\ket{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q}(-1)^{n+l+p+q}n!l!\sqrt{\binom{n+2l+1}{n}\binom{2l}{2l+p-q}}

(324)

\displaystyle\times\innerproduct{\frac{n+l}{2},\frac{n+l}{2}-p;\frac{n+l}{2},q-\frac{n+l}{2}}{l,q-p}

\displaystyle\times\Ket{\frac{n+l}{2},p-\frac{n+l}{2}}_{a}\Ket{\frac{n+l}{2},\frac{n+l}{2}-q}_{b}a^{\dagger}_{1}(c)^{l+q-p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(c)^{l+p-q}\,.

With this expression in hand, we are now in a position to calculate the wave functions.
With our gauge fixing condition, the Wilson line
u_{1}
on the
(a,b)
link is oriented along the
z
-axis.
When evaluating the state above on such a Wilson line via (
23
), each term in the sum will be proportional to the matrix element

\matrixelement{\frac{n+l}{2},\frac{n+l}{2}-p}{r_{\frac{n+l}{2}}(u_1)}{\frac{n+l}{2},\frac{n+l}{2}-q}\,,

(325)

which vanishes unless
p=q
in this gauge.
Let us thus focus on this subsector of the sum.
In it the binomial coefficients can be factored out of the sum, which in turn becomes a resolution of the identity on the space obtained by coupling two spin
(n+l)/2
representations.
Thus, up to proportionality constants, we obtain that

\ell^{n}_{++}\mathscr{L}^{l}_{++}\ket{0}\propto\ket{l,0}_{n}a^{\dagger}_{1}(c)^{l+q-p}a^{\dagger}_{2}(c)^{l+p-q}+\cdots\,.

(326)

The state
\ket{l,0}_{n}
is the total spin
l
state with
0
component in the
z
direction in this coupled space

\displaystyle\ket{l,0}_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p}\Ket{\frac{n+l}{2},p-\frac{n+l}{2}}_{a}\Ket{\frac{n+l}{2},\frac{n+l}{2}-p}_{b}\innerproduct{\frac{n+l}{2},\frac{n+l}{2}-p;\frac{n+l}{2},p-\frac{n+l}{2}}{l,0}\,.

(327)

This evaluates to

\displaystyle\innerproduct{e^{i\omega J_z}}{l,0}_{n}

\displaystyle\propto\sqrt{n+l+1}\sum_{m=-\frac{n+l}{2}}^{\frac{n+l}{2}}e^{i(\omega-\pi)m}\innerproduct{\frac{n+l}{2},m;\frac{n+l}{2},-m}{l,0}\,,

(328)

up to an overall sign.
Due to the symmetry properties of the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients, we can study this function through the purely real function defined by

\displaystyle\phi_{n,l}(\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneq i^{n}\sqrt{n+l+1}\sum_{m=-\frac{n+l}{2}}^{\frac{n+l}{2}}e^{i(\omega-\pi)m}\innerproduct{\frac{n+l}{2},m;\frac{n+l}{2},-m}{l,0}\,,

(329)

to which it is proportional.

For this we need need to now compute the remaining wave function on the stem

\displaystyle\matrixelement{u_2}{(a_1^\dagger(c)a_2^\dagger(c))^la_1^\dagger(d)^{l+m}a_2^\dagger(d)^{l-m}}{0}\propto\innerproduct{u_2}{l,0;l,m}\propto\matrixelement{l,-m}{r_l(u_2)}{l,0}\,,

(330)

Fortunately, this is simple to express in terms of the Euler angles
16
16

16

We will use the
zyz
convention for Euler angles.

(\alpha,\theta,\phi)
of
u_{2}
(see e.g. (37) in
[
97
]
)

\matrixelement{l,0}{r_l(u_2)}{l,m}\propto Y^{l}_{m}(\theta,\phi)\,.

(331)

However, recall that
u_{2}
was any rotation that mapped the axis
\hat{n}
of
u
to the
z
-axis.
If the spherical coordinates of
\hat{n}
are
(\theta,\phi)
, then the rotations that do this are those that have Euler angles
(\alpha,\theta,\phi)
, with
\alpha
parametrizing this
\mathrm{U}(1)
worth of such rotations.
This then finishes the proof of (
57
).
